# Inventory of Rook 0.4 and 0.5, set against PLAN.md

PLAN.md lists this as an open item: an inventory of 0.4's caps, pages and jobs, "so the baseline comes from the system rather than recall". This document is that inventory. It is built from the code: 0.4 on `master`, and 0.5 on `origin/castling`. It is set against PLAN.md as amended on 12 November (amendment 1: Telesthete updated additively, the hub implements the relay, password login). It does not say what to keep or cut. For each thing the old systems do, it says whether the plan covers it, leaves it out on purpose, hands it to the Agents or Workbench plans, or never mentions it.

Read section 1 first. It is the list of everything the plan never mentions.

## How to read this

Every row has exactly one status:

- **Covered: IDs.** A plan line describes the thing. The old system may still do it differently; section 2 lists where.
- **Out by decision.** The row quotes the plan text that leaves it out. Usually that is an item under "Explicitly out this round" or a LATER line. A few rows instead quote a plan line that rejects the thing outright: P7 "No versions card", J6 "No workflow DSL in the hub", or the Build rule "No gates, limits, expiries, approvals or review processes until core is stable. Hardening is a later pass." These rows quote that line, so it is clear where each one comes from.
- **Agents or Workbench.** It belongs to the plans that come after core: the house agent, Rookie, huddles, agent memory, voice, chat bridges, tasks, knowledge or the sessions views. It is not missing; it is listed so it carries forward.
- **Not covered.** PLAN.md does not mention it. Where something might belong to the Agents plan but is not one of the areas above, it is marked Not covered with a note, so that nothing is filed away without a decision.

Row IDs: `CAP-` rows are cap families, and each one counts caps in both versions. Rows ending in `4` (for example `HUB4-`, `PAGE4-`) are 0.4. Rows ending in `5` are 0.5.

### What was counted

| | 0.4 (`master` b93ba4a5) | 0.5 (`origin/castling` db919d7b) |
|---|---|---|
| Caps | 484 declarations: 450 `@capability` methods, 19 registered by name, and 15 in the ESP32 firmware's announce list. 6 of them belong to the conformance reference worker, which leaves 478 product caps. Some names repeat across the desktop, Android and firmware workers (`battery.status`, `hid.*`, `screenshot.*`, `info.*`). | 407 caps from 75 plugin manifests (60 in `rook/plugins`, 15 built into `rook/core2`), with record caps included. 403 distinct names: `stream.list/read/write/close` exist on both the hub and the worker. |
| Cap families in the table | 116 rows (`CAP-01` to `CAP-116`). Their counts add up to 484 and 407. | |
| MCP tools | 38 at most: 26 declared in `rook/band_mcp` and 12 handed over by hub plugins (4 of those depend on settings). No cap sets `tool=True`. See `MCP4-`. | 7: `call`, `caps`, `workers`, `journal`, `holds`, `session`, `stream`. See `MCP5-`. |
| Site pages | 29 dashboard views, plus 11 pages served outside the dashboard (`PAGE4-127` to `PAGE4-137`). The page-to-row map is in section 3.4. | 11: 8 manifest pages and 3 static pages (`PAGE5-01` to `PAGE5-11`). |
| Jobs | 3 ready-made job templates (`JOB4-01` to `JOB4-03`). No jobs are shipped installed. | 4 manifest job templates (`JOB5-01` to `JOB5-04`). Only `attention` installs itself. |
| Other rows | 1,060 rows: MCP tools, services, page features, job-model features and clients. | |

## 1. Not covered: what PLAN.md never mentions

Bake: this is the list to go through line by line. It is grouped by area. The areas come first by how much depends on them in the code (callers, and pages or jobs built on them), so the most structural come first. Each line says what would be lost if the rebuild leaves it out. The row IDs point into section 3.

### 1.1 Reaching people, and agents reaching each other

- **Chat rooms** (`CAP-53`, `MCP4-20`, `MCP4-21`, `HUB4-008`, `HUB4-032`, `HUB4-093`, `HUB4-095`, `HUB4-096`, `HUB5-090`). People and agents share text rooms across the band: start, send, read, delete, presence. A worker can also pop a chat window. In 0.4, 27 other files reference the room caps, the most of any family here. Without them, people and agents have no shared rooms. The Agents plan (P5) brings a chat pane but does not mention rooms.
- **Notify** (`CAP-61`). Jobs and agents send a message to a person on whichever channel reaches them (chat bots, phone). Without it, job failures and agent notices have no generic way out. It is referenced from 22 files.
- **Attention and the Feed** (`CAP-62`, `PAGE4-018`, `PAGE4-019`, `HUB4-127`, `AUX4-058`, `AUX4-061`). Urgent alerts that ring a person's phones, per-phone status, short summaries of notifications, and how each app is sorted (ring me, tell me, just list it, hide it). Without them there is no urgent path to a phone and no Feed of what came in.
- **Phone decision cards and quiet reminders** (`CAP-63`, `APP4-042`, `APP4-043`). Cards on the phone, spoken reminders only when the room is quiet, the phone's quiet feed, its notification rules, and the classifier and channels behind them. Without them, cards and reminders stop. Those cards also carry approvals, which are out.
- **Show on a nearby screen** (`CAP-64`, `HUB4-043`, `HUB4-097`, `PAGE4-131`). An agent says a line and puts one thing on the screen nearest the person (TV or phone browser) through a short-lived public page. Without it, agents cannot put anything in front of a person.

### 1.2 The phone as a worker

The plan covers the phone's install, onboarding and updates (A1 to A3, U4). It says nothing about what the phone can do once it is a worker.

- **Screen reading and input** (`CAP-109`). Read the screen, tap by label, scroll, type, go back, wait for a state. Without it, agents cannot operate phone apps. It is referenced from 20 files.
- **Notifications** (`CAP-111`). Read, dismiss, act on and post the phone's notifications. Without it, agents lose the phone's inbox (20 files).
- **Wake, PIN unlock, keep-awake** (`CAP-106`). Turn the screen on over the lock screen, unlock with the PIN saved in the app, keep the screen awake while agents drive it. Without it, every screen cap above fails on a locked phone.
- **Messages and calls** (`CAP-112`). Read and send SMS, read the call log, place a call.
- **Calendar and contacts** (`CAP-113`).
- **Location** (`CAP-114`).
- **Media playing** (`CAP-115`).
- **Phone controls** (`CAP-105`). Ring to find, vibrate, torch, volume, ringer and Do Not Disturb, clipboard, open links, launch apps, share sheet, settings pages, timers and alarms.
- **Phone screenshots** (`CAP-110`).
- **The app's own worker plumbing** (`APP4-004`, `APP4-005`, `APP4-008`, `APP4-015`, `APP4-020`, `APP4-050`). Reconnect with backoff, reconnect at once when the network changes, plain HTTP only to LAN hosts, a screen that shows every permission grant with a tap to grant it, a roster of workers on the phone, and the settings tabs. Without these, the phone does not reconnect quickly, permissions are hard to grant, and the phone has no view of the band.

### 1.3 The agent's door: MCP and HTTP beyond the five tools

C2 keeps MCP to five generic tools. These are what 0.4 and 0.5 add on top of, or alongside, those five.

- **Help for agents** (`CAP-84`, `HUB4-019`, `MCP4-42`, `APP4-121`, `APP4-134`). The `help` cap gives the core model, then a topic per plugin. Just-in-time `_help` notes appear the first time a tool is used. The Rook skill is served as MCP resources, with its reference pages. Without them, agents lose the built-in explanation of Rook. PL5 only covers a skill shipped with each plugin.
- **Operator guidance for agents** (`HUB4-018`, `HUB4-017`, `MCP4-10`, `PAGE4-049`). Advice the operator can edit: what agents are told when they connect, a tip per tool and per cap, and short tool descriptions. Without it, the owner cannot steer agent behaviour from the hub.
- **Reply shaping** (`MCP4-08`, `MCP4-09`, `HUB4-016`). Vault values are masked in replies, `text=true` returns plain output, and replies are compact. Without them, raw secret values can come back in replies, and replies cost more tokens. L4 scrubs the journal only.
- **Untargeted calls refused, with the workers that could serve** (`MCP4-06`). Without it, an error does not name the workers that have the cap. C3 asks for errors that say what to try next.
- **Identity extras** (`MCP4-01`, `HUB4-002`, `HUB4-005`). `rook_whoami`; an identity that combines token, client, host and working directory; a shared static token. Without them, calls cannot be told apart by machine and directory under one token.
- **Raw secret read for agents and the access log** (`MCP4-16`, `MCP4-17`). Agents can read a secret's raw value, and every read is logged. X3 says only users view values, so this behaviour differs from the plan. The per-secret access log is not in the plan at all.
- **Console search** (`MCP4-29`). Full-text search over every console session ever run.
- **Tools beyond the five** (`MCP4-13`, `MCP4-14`, `MCP4-32`, `MCP5-14`). Plugin-provided tools, caps turned into tools, an install helper for the Claude Code mod, and `session` (narrow a session to some caps and tie it to a task).
- **Connector sign-in for claude.ai** (`CAP-39`, `HUB4-009`, `HUB5-033`, `HUB5-125`). An OAuth front, so the claude.ai web connector can add Rook. Without it, claude.ai cannot connect. T3 covers only copying the first MCP token.
- **Door kill switch** (`CAP-37`). Close the MCP, HTTP or web door so it refuses everything. Without it, there is no single switch that cuts all agent access.
- **Hub summary** (`CAP-35`). One call says what this hub runs: version, plugins, people.
- **HTTP JSON door, stdio shim, mDNS discovery, plugin HTTP routes** (`MCP5-01`, `MCP5-16`, `MCP5-17`, `MCP5-04`). These are 0.5 only. They give non-MCP clients a plain HTTP API, give stdio-only MCP clients a bridge, let devices find the hub on the LAN, and let plugins add endpoints (webhooks, for example).
- **Long calls, why required, sessions, contracts, sub-calls** (`HUB5-025`, `HUB5-015`, `HUB5-014`, `HUB5-023`, `HUB5-024`, `WRK4-029`). These are 0.5 dispatch features, plus one 0.4 worker feature. A call still running after 45 s turns into a stream to follow. A call without a why is refused. Sessions narrow what a caller may call. Contract names route to a chosen provider. A worker can call another cap through the hub.

### 1.4 Jobs beyond "an event match plus one cap call"

J1 to J8 cover the job model. These are the job features both versions have that the plan does not mention.

- **Ready-made job templates** (`CAP-58`, `JOB4-01` to `JOB4-05`, `JOB5-32`, `JOB5-33`, `PAGE4-038`). Plugins ship jobs that a person installs: in 0.4, a security scan every 5 minutes, an attention digest and a camera review. Without them, every job is written by hand, and the nightly scan and the camera review stop.
- **The routines behind those templates** (`CAP-59`). Compare open sessions and logs with the last scan; compare camera frames and ask a vision model about changes.
- **Manual run, "run now", and cancel** (`JOB4-28`, `JOB5-11`, `JOB5-31`). A job with no trigger, started by hand or by an agent, and a running run stopped. The J2 sources do not include "a person pressed run".
- **Run controls** (`JOB4-23`, `JOB4-24`, `JOB4-29`, `JOB4-30`, `JOB5-21` to `JOB5-24`, `JOB5-34`). Step timeouts, retries, success rules, run states beyond success or fail, what happens when a trigger fires during a run, missed runs after downtime, and interrupted runs marked failed at restart.
- **The `after` trigger** (`JOB4-27`). Re-run a job a set time after its own last run.
- **Who a job runs as** (`JOB4-35`, `JOB4-37`, `JOB5-18`). The creator, a named key, or a key kept in the vault, rechecked each run. Editing someone else's job makes the editor its owner. T5 chains tokens to people but says nothing about what identity a job's calls carry.
- **Revisions and history** (`JOB4-44`, `JOB5-29`). Edits are checked against the revision the editor started from, and every change is kept.
- **Repeated-message dedupe** (`JOB4-41`). The same notice to the same person is sent at most once per window.
- **Script jobs and asking a person** (`JOB5-15`, `JOB5-16`, `JOB5-17`). These are 0.5 only: Python jobs run at the hub with a small workflow library, and `job.ask` waits for a person's answer. J6 puts multi-step work in a cap instead.
- **Plugin hooks scheduler** (`JOB5-38`, `JOB5-42`, `JOB5-43`, `JOB5-37`). In 0.5, plugins run `every` and `on` hooks outside jobs. This is how phone notifications and SMS become events.
- **Jobs pages and settings** (`PAGE4-036`, `PAGE4-039`, `PAGE4-040`, `PAGE4-043`, `JOB4-46`, `JOB4-48`). An overview tab, a JSON editor with validation, a schedule helper that reads cron aloud and lists the next fires, job settings (time zone, retention, default agent), and jobs in the terminal panel.
- **Migrated 0.4 jobs** (`JOB5-05`). 0.5 converts each 0.4 job into a disabled job.

### 1.5 Operating desktops and browsers

- **Browser automation** (`CAP-102`). Throwaway Chromium sessions on any worker: an outline of the page, click, type, read, screenshot, run JavaScript, tabs. Without it, agents have no scriptable browser.
- **Keyboard and mouse on the desktop** (`CAP-100`, `APP4-101`). Includes the Linux setup that makes it work on Wayland.
- **Screenshots** (`CAP-101`) and **cameras** (`CAP-103`). Without them, agents cannot see a desktop or a room. The camera review job depends on cameras.
- **Desktop notification** (`CAP-104`).
- **Worker menu and cap form on the Workers page** (`PAGE4-099`, `PAGE4-102`, `PAGE4-103`, `PAGE4-104`). Run any cap from a form built from its schema, take a screenshot, use a one-shot shell dialog, and see device info. P8 attaches stream terminals; these are separate tools.

### 1.6 Managing a worker

- **Restart a worker** (`CAP-12`). Without it, a worker that is up but misbehaving cannot be restarted from the hub or by an agent.
- **The worker's own log** (`CAP-13`, `WRK4-020`, `HUB5-101`). The worker keeps its own record of what it ran. Without it, the hub journal is the only record; nothing on the machine can confirm it.
- **Worker description** (`CAP-14`, `PAGE4-105`, `PAGE4-096`). A one-line note on what a machine is for, which agents see. Without it, agents lose the note.
- **Custom shell caps** (`CAP-15`). A named cap made from a shell template at runtime. Without it, every small cap needs a full plugin.
- **Worker inbox** (`CAP-16`).
- **Rename and repoint a worker** (`CAP-28`, `PAGE4-106`). Rename it, or move it to another hub, without reinstalling. G1 has the hub mint monikers; the plan does not say whether a person can rename a worker.
- **Commit-confirmed config** (`CAP-22`, `HUB4-181`, `WRK4-056`). A pushed config restarts the worker and reverts by itself unless confirmed. Without it, a bad config pushed to a remote machine stays.
- **Config and secrets for services outside the hub** (`CAP-24`, `HUB4-175`). The voice server and the embedder fetch their own settings with a scoped token. Without it, those services have no way to get config.
- **Update handling on the worker** (`WRK4-048`, `WRK4-050`). A build that was rolled back is not tried again, and an update waits while terminals or agent sessions are running. Without these, an update can interrupt live work, or retry a build that already failed.
- **Machine id fact and hash-only announces** (`WRK4-013`, `WRK4-009`). The machine id tells which workers share a host. Hash-only announces save bandwidth, but the plan asks for full state on every heartbeat.

### 1.7 Devices and drivers

D1 to D8 cover the device mechanism. No driver is named, and PL4 only expects that "device drivers are expected to be the first canned plugins".

- **LG webOS TV** (`CAP-91`), **Roku** (`CAP-92`), **HDMI-CEC** (`CAP-93`), **PiKVM** (`CAP-94`, `APP4-096`), **Deluge/torrents** (`CAP-95`), **Android over adb** (`CAP-96`). Each is a driver someone uses today. Without them, those devices drop off the band.
- **Network discovery** (`CAP-89`). Find devices on a worker's network (SSDP, mDNS, adb). Without it, devices are added by typing addresses.
- **Pair on add, and the action engine** (`HUB5-069`, `HUB5-074`). These are 0.5 only. A device's pairing step runs when it is added, and manifest caps map to built-in exec, HTTP, TCP, WebSocket and file actions without plugin code.

### 1.8 Site features beyond the eight core pages

- **Start page and widgets** (`PAGE4-021` to `PAGE4-025`, `PAGE4-027`, `HUB4-192`, `HUB4-193`, `JOB4-09`, `PAGE5-21`). A personal page of bookmarks, news feeds, notes and widgets added by plugins.
- **Agent-made pages** (`CAP-86`, `PAGE5-12`, `PAGE5-13`, `PAGE5-16`, `PAGE5-18` to `PAGE5-22`, `PAGE5-25`, `PAGE5-29`, `PAGE5-30`, `PAGE5-31`, `PAGE5-33`). These are 0.5 only: agents make and change site pages from HTML or from ready-made components, in sandboxed frames, with archive and restore and a note on who made each page. P1 covers plugin pages only.
- **Themes** (`PAGE4-011`, `HUB4-191`) and **pause artwork** (`PAGE4-013`).
- **Phone layout of the site** (`PAGE4-008`, `PAGE4-009`). A bottom tab bar, and tables that stack on narrow screens. Without them, the site is hard to use on a phone.
- **Small page conveniences** (`PAGE4-004`, `PAGE4-005`, `PAGE4-006`, `PAGE4-010`, `PAGE4-017`). Old links that still open, the (i) description on each page, the live dot with a worker count, tap to copy, and the "manual changed" banner.
- **Workers page extras** (`PAGE4-090`, `PAGE4-091`, `PAGE4-093`, `PAGE4-100`). Group by OS and sort, filter by band and text, device-kind icons, and a map link for locations.
- **Settings extras** (`PAGE4-074`, `PAGE4-075`, `PAGE4-077`, `PAGE4-080`, `PAGE4-081`, `HUB4-179`). Search every setting, see where each value comes from, a status overview, per-person preferences, and per-band worker defaults.
- **Plugin presets** (`PAGE4-114`, `HUB4-144`, `JOB4-08`, `WRK4-018`, `HUB4-112`). Groups such as "What do you want Rook to do?" that switch plugins on together and set defaults for new workers.
- **Account extras** (`PAGE4-055`, `PAGE4-057`, `PAGE4-059`, `PAGE4-063`, `HUB4-102` to `HUB4-104`, `PAGE5-03`). Profile and avatar, invitations, your bands, linking or merging accounts, and changing your own password.
- **Help page with the user manual** (`CAP-85`, `PAGE4-126`, `HUB4-041`, `HUB4-124`, `JOB4-55`). Without it, the manual is not reachable inside the system.
- **Model providers** (`CAP-83`, `PAGE4-047`, `HUB5-012`). Which LLM providers the hub can use, their models and health, and one completion call. Hub-side code (decide, routines, 0.5 jobs and security-ml) uses it. It may belong to the Agents plan.
- **Decision model and screen driving** (`CAP-82`, `JOB4-61`, `HUB4-176`). A local model answers typed questions and drives a screen toward a goal. It may belong to the Agents plan.

### 1.9 Installing, running and updating the hub

The plan covers worker and client updates (U1 to U9). It says almost nothing about the hub's own life.

- **First-run setup** (`PAGE4-127`, `HUB4-062`, `HUB5-123`). A wizard, or `init`, that sets the hub's address, the first credentials and the plugin presets. Without it, a new hub has no guided start.
- **Hub install paths** (`APP4-112`, `APP4-113`, `APP4-115`, `APP4-072`, `PAGE4-136`, `HUB4-080`, `HUB4-081`). A one-line hub installer, Docker and Compose, a desktop "install a hub" wizard, the CLI installer, an override that points workers at the relay's own WebSocket listener, and dev and test scripts. (The separate relay installer, `APP4-114`, is covered by the amended Topology line, which makes the hub the relay.) Without them, a hub is set up by hand. The plan only says that "uptime is the host's problem" and names an Oracle VM.
- **Hub self-update and rollback** (`CAP-31`, `AUX4-049`, `PAGE4-119`, `AUX4-085` to `AUX4-090`, `WRK4-105`, `WRK4-106`). Signed hub releases, deploy with a dead-man auto-rollback, and history and pruning. Without them, every hub update is manual and cannot roll back by itself.
- **Release discovery from GitHub** (`CAP-30`, `AUX4-045`, `AUX4-046`, `AUX4-056`, `PAGE4-124`). The hub polls GitHub releases and checks downloads against SHA256SUMS. Without it, artefact sets are loaded into the hub by hand (U6 does not say how they arrive).
- **Update modes and safety** (`AUX4-047`, `PAGE4-118`, `AUX4-051`, `AUX4-052`, `AUX4-054`, `AUX4-094`). Off, tell me, or install inside a window; canaries and health gates; floors that name the devices a release would cut off; firmware listing; pinned dependencies.
- **Health, watchdog and restart** (`HUB4-036`, `HUB4-059`, `HUB4-037`, `HUB4-177`, `HUB4-038`, `HUB4-039`, `HUB4-180`, `PAGE4-076`, `WRK4-083`). Health endpoints, a probe every minute with Telegram alerts, memory diagnostics, and restart from Settings. Without them, nobody hears when the hub breaks.
- **Hub address handling** (`HUB4-063`, `HUB4-064`, `HUB4-053`, `HUB4-057`, `HUB4-067`, `PAGE4-133`, `PAGE4-137`). How the hub decides its public URL and scheme, its serve flags, plugin URL mounts, an installer log that never records join codes, plain-text help for `curl /`, and the desktop update feed.
- **Shared dashboard password** (`HUB4-060`). T4 allows only user login or a token.
- **Secrets from the hub CLI** (`HUB5-044`).

### 1.10 Joining, bands and moving machines the 0.4 and 0.5 way

The plan defines bands as gateway MACs (B1, B2) and registration as knock and approve (R2, R5). Both old versions do it differently: bands are pre-shared keys, and devices join with pairing codes or certificates. These pieces have no plan line because the plan replaces them. Section 2 sets the two sides out.

- **Bands as pre-shared keys** (`HUB4-084` to `HUB4-087`, `HUB4-029`, `PAGE4-069`, `PAGE4-072`, `APP4-011`, `APP4-012`, `APP4-091`). Create a band from a five-word key, join by key, rotate or revoke a key, and pick an active band on the phone.
- **Moving workers between bands** (`CAP-33`, `HUB4-114`, `WRK4-064`, `PAGE4-068`, `PAGE4-107`). Signed migrations, with progress, resume and cancel.
- **Pairing codes and device-code sign-in** (`APP4-010`, `APP4-105`, `HUB4-109`, `PAGE4-058`, `HUB4-108`, `HUB4-111`). A six-character code, or a code shown by the installer and approved in the browser. Band config can be downloaded, and the phone's roster refreshes. A2 says "No code entry".
- **Device certificates and their renewal** (`APP4-109`, `WRK4-066`). See also the expiry rows marked Out.
- **Signed hub roles and announce** (`HUB4-154`, `HUB5-003`, `HUB5-019`). The hub proves it is the hub with a signed grant. 0.5 also signs a ticket for every call.
- **WebSocket bridge for workers** (`HUB4-035`). The plan gives the WebSocket fallback to Telesthete.

### 1.11 Journal, records and settings extras (mostly 0.5)

- **Tamper-evident journal** (`HUB5-035`, `HUB5-036`, `HUB5-008`). A hash chain per project, triggers that refuse rewrites, and an hourly chain check. Without them, nobody can show the journal was not edited.
- **Archive and restore of records** (`HUB5-083`). A soft delete. K4 says nothing is auto-deleted but does not mention archiving.
- **Per-person and per-worker settings** (`HUB5-076`, `HUB5-077`).
- **Plugin change records and the manifest validator** (`HUB5-065`, `HUB5-067`). A change record is kept per rollout, and breaking manifest changes need a major version.
- **Stream export** (`HUB5-087`). Copies a bounded terminal buffer into a durable stream.

### 1.12 Transport and wire details

- **Large replies and bundles over Drop** (`WRK4-036`, `WRK4-044`, `HUB5-113`). Big replies, and update bundles pushed in band, are pulled in chunks. U2 has the bootstrap download over HTTPS.
- **Reply compression and feature negotiation** (`WRK4-035`), and **addressed routes** (`HUB5-112`). It may be that Telesthete owns these (Layering); the code does not settle it.

### 1.13 Dongles and firmware

R3, U5 and P9 describe the ESP32-S3 dongle the plan wants. The 0.4 dongle firmware does other things as well:

- **BLE keyboard, storage mode and file drop, LCD, local HTTP admin, serial CLI, reboot to bootloader** (`CAP-99`, `APP4-081` to `APP4-085`, `APP4-087`, `APP4-089`, `APP4-090`). Without them, the dongle loses its wireless keyboard, USB drive, screen and local admin. The plan's button installs a worker; the 0.4 button switches storage mode.
- **RP2040 USB dongle** (`CAP-97`, `APP4-093`). A cheaper USB keyboard and mouse driven by a host worker, with no band membership of its own.
- **Standalone KVM MCP server** (`APP4-094`). 19 tools that drive the ESP32 dongle directly, outside Rook.
- **Dongle smoke test** (`APP4-095`).

### 1.14 The `rook` command line

- **Terminal panel** (`WRK4-097`). Live roster, run caps from forms, switch plugins, manage custom caps.
- **Local commands** (`WRK4-111`, `WRK4-116`, `WRK4-117`, `WRK4-118`, `WRK4-067`). `rook status`, `rook plugins`, `rook logs`, and `rook doctor`, which checks Python, keys, clock and band link. They talk to the worker over a local link. Without them, a machine cannot diagnose itself.
- **Hub access from the CLI** (`WRK4-120`, `WRK4-122`, `WRK4-069`). Save a token or login, then reach the hub through it. T3 says humans never type tokens.
- **CLI install and extras** (`APP4-111`, `WRK4-004`, `WRK4-108`, `WRK4-121`, `WRK4-006`, `WRK4-103`, `WRK4-110`, `APP4-116`). The CLI installer and launcher, skill install, shell completion, a warning when saved state beats flags, the two-process mode, the retired `tmux` notice, and `start.bat`.
- **Termux install path** (`APP4-102`). The worker installed inside Termux on Android.

### 1.15 Desktop app

- **Dashboard window, tray menu, single instance, settings window, diagnostics** (`APP4-061`, `APP4-062`, `APP4-073`, `APP4-074`, `APP4-075`, `APP5-14`). U2 and T2 say what the desktop app is. They do not mention its window and tray behaviour.

### 1.16 Build, test and release tooling

- **Conformance suite** (`APP4-136`, `APP4-137`). Offline test vectors and a live harness that any worker implementation can run. W1 says any implementation that speaks the protocol is a worker; the plan does not say how that is checked.
- **CI and release workflows** (`AUX4-104`, `AUX4-106`, `AUX4-107`, `AUX4-100`). Tests, installer checks, and signed and attested releases.
- **Generators and checks** (`AUX4-097`, `AUX4-098`, `AUX4-099`, `AUX4-101`, `AUX4-102`). The settings and skill references are generated from code, the manual is checked for coverage, and MCP token budgets and diagnostics are measured.
- **Fresh-install harness** (`APP4-119`). Installs on throwaway VMs and an emulator, counting the human touches.
- **Claude Code mod extras** (`APP4-139`, `APP4-147`). The Bands tab and the install path. The rest of the mod is in Agents or Workbench.
- **Records lookup by spoken name** (`AUX4-004`). Reads accept one clear near-miss.

### 1.17 Switching between 0.4 and 0.5

These are 0.5 only, and they matter only if a migration happens:

- **0.4 to 0.5 migration and rehearsal tools** (`HUB5-119`, `HUB5-120`, `HUB5-122`).
- **Version switch in the phone and desktop apps** (`APP5-01`, `APP5-04`, `APP5-07`, `APP5-11`).

### 1.18 Implementation details (no capability is lost)

These rows are listed so that every row is accounted for. They are how the old code was built, not things a person or agent uses: `HUB4-051`, `HUB4-052`, `HUB4-054` to `HUB4-056`, `HUB4-150`, `HUB4-152`, `HUB4-186`, `HUB4-188` to `HUB4-190`, `HUB4-195`, `HUB4-126`, `WRK4-016`, `WRK4-019`, `WRK4-040`, `WRK4-068`, `WRK4-077`, `WRK4-080` to `WRK4-082`, `APP4-045`, `APP4-054`, `HUB5-115`, `APP5-20`, `JOB4-56` (settings saved by another hub process applied live). Also `HUB4-024` and `HUB4-025`, the internal endpoints between the dashboard and the MCP side.


## 2. Where the plan and the old systems pull in different directions

In each item below, the plan covers the area, but someone used to 0.4 or 0.5 would notice a difference. Both sides are stated, with no preference. Where 0.4 and 0.5 agree with each other, they are given once.

### Workers, identity and heartbeat

- **Who mints identity (W7, G1).** Plan: at first approval the hub mints a GUID and a three-word moniker. The hub keeps a seen-workers record (MACs, hostnames, keys), and flags a likely rekey. 0.4: each worker makes its own `worker_id` (a uuid4 on desktops, random hex on the firmware), and the phone's name defaults to its model. Three-word names exist only for consoles, tasks, jobs, rooms and similar. 0.5: a 32-hex id from enrolment, with the name defaulting to the hostname. A different key is refused until the worker re-enrols. Neither version has monikers for workers or a seen-workers record.
- **The heartbeat (H1, H2, H4, H5).** Plan: the heartbeat is the only announce, and every one carries full state, including a fixed health block. The hub sets the interval for each worker and sends it back on connect. History is kept raw for a day and hourly for a month. 0.4: announces go every 30 s on the worker's own timer (±20 %), and can be set by a flag or pushed config. Hash-only announces, which drop caps, are optional. Health is a block per plugin (battery, TV state, terminals), not CPU, RAM and disk. Nothing stores history; the page shows live bars over a 60 s window. 0.5: a separate announce on connect carries the full state, and the heartbeat carries only id, core, a manifest hash and status. The interval is a fixed 30 s, facts are OS, arch, CPUs and Python, there is no health block, and there is no history.
- **Offline (H3).** Plan: three missed heartbeats mark the worker offline, and its record and caps are kept. 0.4: the MCP roster drops a worker after 90 s of silence. 0.5: offline after 90 s, which is about three beats.
- **The hub and the relay (Topology, as amended).** Plan: hub and spoke, calls route through the hub, and the Rook hub implements the Telesthete relay as its base. 0.4: the hub is itself a worker named `rook` on the band, and a separate Telesthete relay process (installed on its own, with its own settings) carries traffic. Calls that come from the band are held to read-only. 0.5: the hub joins the band through a relay it is given (`init --relay`); it does not implement one.

### Bands and registration

- **What a band is (B1 to B3, B6).** Plan: a band is a set of gateway MACs the user has named. Membership is automatic, and unnamed networks show as roaming. 0.4 and 0.5: a band is a pre-shared key. The person creates it by name and key and joins it explicitly. Workers move between bands with signed migrations, and keys rotate. 0.5 lets one worker belong to several bands and hubs. Neither version matches on gateway MAC or has a roaming state.
- **Joining (R1, R2, R4, R5).** Plan: one command with the hub URL as the only input. The worker knocks with a public key and sits pending, showing its name and offered caps read-only, and anyone approves it from any client, including an agent through MCP. Approval signs the key; there is no re-pairing and no expiry. 0.4: the install command carries a six-character pairing code that lasts 5 minutes, or starts a device-code sign-in that is approved in the browser. The hub CA issues a 30-day certificate that the worker renews; the worker can boot offline for 1 hour on saved credentials; the band PSK still seals traffic. There is no pending queue. 0.5: a person signs a device in with a one-time code, a QR code or a device code, and must be allowed to enrol. The pending view shows device codes, not offered caps, and approval is over HTTP only, with no MCP tool.
- **Revoking (R5, W7).** Plan: either side revokes the key; the identity, owner, config and history stay. 0.4: the worker is banned by name or id on a denylist, and a signed deauth order parks it; reviving it needs local action on the machine.
- **Join tokens (R3, T3).** Plan: JWT join tokens that last minutes and work once; humans never type tokens. 0.4: people type pairing codes (`rook pair`, the phone, the dongle script), and `rook login --token` saves a typed token. 0.5: the phone and the desktop ask the person to paste a hub token.

### Android and desktop clients

- **Android onboarding (A1 to A3).** Plan: the hub stamps its own URL into the APK signing block on download. The person signs in with Google; the hub matches or provisions a user and issues a token scoped worker and client. There is no code entry, and approval is implicit. 0.4: the server URL, default hub, Google client id, voice URL and wake word are baked in at build time. Google sign-in goes to a separate "Rook server" that returns a list of bands, each with a hub and a PSK, and the person picks the active band. A pairing code is the alternative, and hub, PSK and name are editable fields. 0.5: the person types the hub address, or scans a QR one-time code, or shows a device code for someone to approve. There is no Google sign-in.
- **Android updates (U4, U6, U7).** Plan: the hub serves the new APK and the app prompts. The hub holds one artefact set per channel (dev, stable), and nothing updates on its own. 0.4: the app checks every 6 hours with automatic updates on by default, and installs silently where Android allows. The phone chooses stable or beta. The APK is signed with the app signing key, not by the hub (U9).
- **Bootstrap and core (U1, U2).** Plan: every client is a small generic bootstrap plus a swappable core, and the bootstrap never speaks Telesthete. 0.4: the APK is one unit (Kotlin app, embedded Python worker and the protocol library), so a new core needs a whole new APK. On desktops the `.pyz` runs directly under systemd or a scheduled task, and the self-update logic lives inside the worker. 0.5: the worker picks and runs its own core (`boot.py`); there is no separate bootstrap.
- **Phone plugins (PL3).** Plan: the hub pushes worker parts of a plugin to the right worker forms, hot-loaded. 0.4: Android plugins are compiled into the APK.
- **Desktop app (T2, U2).** Plan: the desktop app is worker and client at once, under one token with several scopes. 0.4: the tray app is a client only. It signs in with the dashboard cookie or a password, and starts or stops a worker that the installer set up separately. Its own updates open a GitHub download.

### Updates and releases

- **Rollout (U7).** Plan: rollout is a job, and nothing updates on its own by default. 0.4: worker bundles converge on their own. The installer passes `--update-url`, so every desktop worker polls every 300 s, and the hub pushes the newest signed bundle every 20 s (`updates.push` defaults to on). The hub's own release install defaults to "tell me", and auto mode installs inside a maintenance window. Staged rollouts use canaries and health gates. 0.5: rollout is a cap (`update.rollout`) with canaries and batches, not a job.
- **Core swap and rollback (U3).** Plan: start the new core, hand over, exit the old one. If the new core does not heartbeat within a window the hub sets, the bootstrap restarts the old core and reports over HTTPS. 0.4: the worker swaps `band-worker.pyz` in place (keeping `.prev`), restarts through the service manager (stop, then start), and judges health with fixed constants (a 180 s band gate and boot counters). It reports the result on its announce. 0.5: the trial window is an environment variable.
- **Where releases come from and who signs them (U6, U9).** Plan: the hub holds one artefact set per channel, dev and stable, and builds are signed by the hub. 0.4: the hub polls GitHub releases, trusts SHA256SUMS and optional attestations, and signs bundles with an operator update key. Channels are stable and prerelease, and the APK has stable and beta. The desktop feed points at GitHub assets.
- **Compatibility (U8).** Plan: compatibility is carried by the protocol version and the bootstrap contract version. 0.4: `release.json` carries compatibility floors and a "breaking" flag, and a wizard names the devices a release would cut off.
- **Build names (G2).** Plan: a two-word moniker alongside semver. 0.4: the build number plus `adjective.noun` (for example `1161.lively.crumpet`) alongside 0.4.x; worker builds are `count.hash`.
- **Versions view (P7, U6).** Plan: the Workers page shows stragglers, and there is no versions card. 0.4: a versions tile on Workers, and Settings > Updates lists every member's version.

### Devices

- **Where drivers run (D2, D6, D7).** Plan: drivers run at the hub and turn calls into network primitives. The hub polls device status through the route, and device config lives at the hub. 0.4: drivers are worker plugins (webOS, adb, devkit). They poll locally, report in the worker's heartbeat, store their definitions on the worker, and each device shows with its own worker id and heartbeat. 0.5: an appliance registry at the hub routes to delegates, and an action engine maps caps to exec, HTTP, TCP, WebSocket or file actions. No per-device status polling and no per-worker opt-out from serving devices (D8) were found.
- **Primitives (D3).** Plan: every worker exposes HTTP request and one-shot TCP and UDP send. 0.4 and 0.5: no such primitive caps. 0.4 drivers talk to devices directly; 0.5 manifest actions do the HTTP or TCP work inside the driver.

### Caps, MCP, streams

- **MCP surface (C2).** Plan: five generic tools, and caps are discovered, not registered as tools. 0.4: up to 38 tools, plus skill resources and a way to turn caps into tools. A separate dongle MCP server has 19 more. 0.5: the five plus `holds` and `session`, an `elevate` flag on `call`, and long calls that turn into streams.
- **Streams (S2 to S4).** Plan: workers own streams; the hub fans out to any number of clients; streams never time out. 0.4: finished terminals and processes are dropped after 900 s, and live counts are capped (8 terminals, 16 processes). The hub-side fan-out allows 32 followed streams and 16 viewers, and one viewer types at a time. Idle followed streams stop after 60 s. Console rooms are hub-side copies filled by polling the worker, and a room closes when its worker stops answering. 0.5: terminals and processes keep only the newest 256 KiB; older output is dropped.
- **Stream text (S3).** Plan: a free-text title and description. 0.4: a title is required at open and a summary at close, and closed rooms are frozen.
- **Terminal on the worker card (P8).** Plan: streams attach inline as a terminal on the worker card. 0.4: live terminals are on the Sessions page, and the card's "Shell" runs one `shell.exec` call per command.
- **Limits (Build rule).** Plan: no limits until core is stable. 0.4: a 300 s default cap timeout, a cap on concurrent calls that sheds the rest with "busy", and a login throttle. Both versions have job-run caps and dedupe windows.

### Jobs and events

- **What a job is (J1, J6).** Plan: an event match plus one cap call with fixed arguments; multi-step work is a cap; no workflow DSL in the hub. 0.4: a job is a JSON graph of 10 step kinds (cap, fanout, tool, wait, notify, join, noop, agent, ask, huddle), with branching, joins and an expression language, guardrails and locks. 0.5: a job is one of a cap call, an agent prompt, an owner's Python script run at the hub with a workflow library, or a plugin's script.
- **Triggers and the event bus (J2, J8).** Plan: one event bus whose sources are hub state changes, schedules, token-scoped webhooks, workers and agents; events are journaled. 0.4: triggers are cron, at, after (the same job) and manual. There are no webhooks anywhere in 0.4 and no event triggers. 0.5: cron, every, events with a filter, manual, and webhooks with an HMAC per job. There are no "token used" or "band change" events. Events go to hooks and pages but are not journaled.
- **Chaining (J3 to J5).** Plan: completions emit success or fail, jobs chain on them, cycles are warned about, and a chain shares a run id. 0.4 and 0.5: jobs cannot trigger on another job's completion, and there is no cycle view and no shared chain id.
- **Delete (J7, K6).** Plan: a hard delete, with past runs kept in the journal; jobs stay real tables. 0.4: delete also removes the job's runs, history and notices, and run history is pruned after 30 days. 0.5: "delete" is an archive that can be restored. Jobs and runs are records, and runs are erased after 30 days.

### Secrets

- **Syntax and scope (X1, X2).** Plan: `secret:name`, scoped to a worker, a band, the hub or a string, resolved worker, then band, then hub. 0.4 and 0.5: `{{secret:name}}` with flat names. In 0.4 the syntax also works inside console input.
- **Who sees values (X3).** Plan: only users view values, through a reveal button on the Secrets page that is journaled; agents and jobs can create secrets. 0.4: the Secrets page never shows a value, while `rook_secret get` hands raw values to agents (logged). 0.5: there is no Secrets page; `secret.*` caps are owner-only, and reveal and delete are hard stops.
- **Vault key (X4).** Plan: the vault key is separate from hub data and unlocked at start. 0.4: a key file next to the database. 0.5: the data key is sealed under a root key and a recovery key, both inside the data folder.

### Journal

- **One journal (L1 to L3).** Plan: one flat table, searchable, with retention as a setting. 0.4: calls are in a journal capped at 20,000 rows and pruned on write. Account changes, settings history, trust history, approvals and the event log each have their own store, and the event log drops rows after 30 days. There is no journal page. 0.5: one journal table with a fixed 90-day retention, hash chains per project, and a call with no why is refused.
- **Scrubbing and redaction (L4 to L6).** Plan: scrub known secret shapes and vault values on write; a redact cap; a `secret.detected` event. 0.4: masks vault values everywhere (replies, chat, knowledge, consoles), with no shape patterns, and redacts journal rows when a secret is set. Neither version has a redact cap or a `secret.detected` event.

### Records

- **Records as caps (K2).** Plan: no raw record caps; each plugin writes its own typed caps. 0.5: every declared record type generates `<plugin>.<record>.<verb>` caps automatically.
- **Storage (K1, K3, K7, K5).** Plan: one records table with indexed columns, a JSON body, a links table and a full-text index; blobs are a record type. 0.4: knowledge and work live in their own `knowledge.db` schema. 0.5: one table per record type, history and full-text search per type, and no blob type.
- **Auto-deletion (K4, Explicitly out).** Plan: nothing is auto-deleted. 0.4: huddles archived for 90 days are deleted, voice state after 7 days, attention alerts expire, the Claude Code mirror spools after a week, and expired device certificates are removed. 0.5: run records after 30 days, the general journal chain after 90, old backups beyond `keep`, and ended sessions hourly.

### Tokens and front doors

- **Token format (T1, T2).** Plan: JWT for everything, with purpose, expiry and scope as claims. 0.4: opaque stored tokens with an identity, owner, expiry and last-used, plus one shared static token with no owner. There is no MCP tool for minting. 0.5: opaque tokens, stored hashed, each acting for a person through a named agent, with no scope or expiry claims.
- **Front doors (T4).** Plan: user login for humans, by password for now, and tokens for everything else; nothing else authenticates. 0.4 also has a shared dashboard password with HTTP Basic, device-code login, device certificates, the voice service's own admin login and keys, link codes for Telegram and Discord, and an attribution check that lets a call through "unverified" if the checker crashes. 0.5 also has an OAuth front, one-time and device codes, HMAC webhook routes, and plugin routes with `auth: none`.
- **Auth page (T6).** Plan: users, and tokens with scope, owner and last-used. 0.5: a Tokens page without scope or last-used; people are a read-only table defined in `hub.yaml`.

### Config and settings

- **Config direction (CF1, CF2).** Plan: each worker has a config record at the hub; clients fetch it after connecting and write user changes back; the hub is the source of truth. 0.4: the hub pushes config to the worker, which applies it commit-confirmed with auto-revert and keeps it in its own `config.json`. Flags and environment on the hub override stored values and lock them. User edits in the phone app are not written back. 0.5: settings live at the hub, on the worker, or per person; there is no per-worker config record.
- **Settings page (P4).** Plan: hub name, channel, retention, provisioning mode and Plugins. 0.4: every setting of the hub, bands, workers, plugins and the user, from a schema, with sources and history. Channel is on Updates, plugins have their own page, and there is no provisioning-mode setting.

### The site

- **Technology (P1).** Plan: Vue 3 without a build step; plugin UI and core UI are the same mechanism. 0.4: plain JavaScript modules, no Vue, and plugins can add only start-page widgets. 0.5: a plain-JavaScript shell that opens each page in a sandboxed frame.
- **Navigation and pins (P2, P6).** Plan: a Pinned strip, then segments that appear when something installs a page into them; pins on every page header. 0.4: five fixed sections with 29 hard-coded links. 0.5: sections come from manifests. Neither version has pins.
- **The eight core pages (P4).** Plan: Workers, Auth, Secrets, Jobs, Data (Journal, Records), Install and Settings. 0.4: 29 views. There is no Journal, Records or Data page, users are split across People and Account, and Bands, Plugins and Updates are separate pages. 0.5: Workers, Tokens, Journal and Settings. There are no Secrets, Jobs, Records or Install pages.
- **Workers page (P4, P7, P3).** Plan: grouped by band, devices as a filter, pending approvals, band rename and merge on the group header, share and plugin opt-out on the row, a stats strip (online, stale, pending, devices), a heartbeats graph, and list, grid and 3D views. 0.4: grouped by OS by default, with devices mixed in and no pending approvals. Band rename is on the Bands page, there is no merge and no shares, and plugin opt-out is in Settings. The stats are workers, online and stale plus a versions tile. 0.5: a plain table.
- **Install page (P9).** Plan: the APK as a public link with a QR code; installers with OS icons and no macOS; the dongle flashed from the browser over WebSerial. 0.4: a pairing code plus bash (labelled "Linux / macOS") and PowerShell commands. The APK link has no QR code, no desktop installers are listed, and there is no WebSerial; the dongle is built with a script.
- **Chat pane (P5).** Plan: core has no chat pane. 0.4: the house-agent dock is built into the core page, and the agent can act on the page. 0.5: the dock is in the core shell when the agents package is installed.
- **Logo and scene (P10).** 0.5 shows only the text "Rook"; 0.4 has the logo and the three.js scene.

### Plugins

- **Install and reach (PL2, PL4).** Plan: install from a folder or a URL, by a user in Settings or by an agent through MCP; no plugin hooks into routing, auth or the journal. 0.4: plugins bundled with the release or installed with pip, needing a restart; the marketplace install is not implemented. 0.5: owners push plugins over HTTP, with no Settings install and no MCP tool. Plugins add HTTP routes with their own auth, gates in dispatch, contracts and hooks.

### Gates, permissions and federation

- **No gates this round (Build rules, Explicitly out).** Plan: no gates, limits, expiries or approvals except knock and approve. 0.4: policy, trust map, approvals, hard stops, access grants and job guardrails all run. 0.5: rights, then gates, then holds, then a signed ticket for every call. Canaries, taint, a machine-learning gate, sacred machines and elevation leases are all built. `hub.security` defaults to off, which skips gates and holds but keeps rights, tickets and the why requirement.
- **Owner is a fact (W6, F8).** Plan: owner is a fact, not a gate, and the share cap list is the only permission. 0.4: owner and member roles plus grants decide what a person reaches. 0.5: presets (owner, adult, child, guest), agent grants, session narrowing, page grants and private records.
- **Federation (F4 to F8).** Plan: share one worker hub to hub, with a cap list and an invite link. 0.5: band invites and signed memberships, so a worker can belong to several hubs (the plan lists multi-hub per worker as out). Neither version shares a single worker with a cap list.


## 3. The full table

Columns: the row ID; what it is; where it lives (0.4, 0.5 or both, with a path); what it does, in one plain line; the status. Paths are relative to the repository root. In 0.4, `rook/hub/plugins/` is a hub plugin and `rook/worker/plugins/` is a worker plugin. In 0.5, `rook/plugins/<name>/` is the plugin's folder and its `plugin.yaml`.

### 3.1 Cap families (0.4 and 0.5)

Each row is one family. The count column gives caps in 0.4 / caps in 0.5, and the columns add up to 484 / 407. A family that is mostly covered but has one member doing something the plan never mentions gets a row of its own for that member.

| ID | What it is | Where | What it does | Caps 0.4 / 0.5 | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CAP-01 | Core worker caps: `shell.exec`, `shell.which` | 0.4: `rook/worker/plugins/shell.py`<br>0.5: `rook/plugins/shell/` | Run a command and get code/stdout/stderr; find an executable on PATH. | 2 / 2 | Covered: PL7 |
| CAP-02 | Core worker caps: `shell.env.*` (read the environment) | 0.4: `rook/worker/plugins/shell.py`<br>0.5: `rook/plugins/shell/` | Read one or all environment variables; values pushed as secrets read `***`. | 2 / 1 | Covered: PL7 |
| CAP-03 | Core worker caps: `shell.open` (terminal as a stream) | 0.5: `rook/plugins/shell/` | Open a shell under a PTY and return a stream handle. | 0 / 1 | Covered: S1, S2, P8 |
| CAP-04 | Core worker caps: `file.*` | 0.4: `rook/worker/plugins/file_ops.py`<br>0.5: `rook/plugins/file/` | Read, write, list, grep and stat files on the worker (base64 for bytes). | 5 / 5 | Covered: PL7 |
| CAP-05 | Core worker caps: Liveness and host facts: `info.host`, `info.uptime`, `info.ping`, `worker.ping` | 0.4: `firmware/src/telesthete.cpp`, `rook/worker/devices.py`, `rook/worker/plugins/info.py`<br>0.5: `rook/core2/builtin/worker.yaml` | Hostname/OS/Python/pid, process uptime, pong. | 7 / 1 | Covered: H1, PL7 |
| CAP-06 | Core worker caps: `worker.status` | 0.4: `android/app/src/main/python/rook_android/worker_runtime.py`, `rook/worker/plugins/selfupdate.py`<br>0.5: `rook/core2/builtin/worker.yaml` | One read of the worker: build, version, update state, bands, plugins. | 2 / 1 | Covered: H1 |
| CAP-07 | Core worker caps: `battery.status` | 0.4: `android/…/rook_android/plugins/battery_android.py`, `rook/worker/plugins/battery.py`<br>0.5: `rook/plugins/battery-android/`, `rook/plugins/battery/` | Battery percent, charging, plugged, temperature. | 2 / 2 | Covered: H4, PL7 |
| CAP-08 | Core worker caps: `caps.describe` | 0.4: `rook/hub/node.py`, `rook/worker/core.py`, `rook/worker/devices.py` | Argument schema, docstring and risk for every cap on a worker or the hub. | 3 / 0 | Covered: W3, C2 |
| CAP-09 | Core worker caps: Process sessions: `proc.*` | 0.4: `rook/worker/plugins/proc.py` | Start a background process, read its output by cursor, write stdin, signal, close, list live and recently finished. | 6 / 0 | Covered: S1, S2, S4 |
| CAP-10 | Core worker caps: Terminals: `work.stream.*` (open, read, write, resize, signal, close, list) | 0.4: `rook/worker/plugins/terminals.py` | A PTY terminal on the worker that agents and the browser read and type into. | 7 / 0 | Covered: S1, S2, S4, P8 |
| CAP-11 | Core worker caps: Streams: `stream.*` on the worker | 0.5: `rook/core2/builtin/stream.yaml` | Read, write, list and close any stream the worker owns (terminals, processes, sessions). | 0 / 4 | Covered: S1, S2, S4, S5 |
| CAP-12 | Core worker caps: Worker restart: `worker.restart` | 0.4: `android/app/src/main/python/rook_android/worker_runtime.py`, `rook/worker/plugins/selfupdate.py`<br>0.5: `rook/core2/builtin/worker.yaml` | Restart the worker process in place on request (kills its live terminals). | 2 / 1 | Not covered |
| CAP-13 | Core worker caps: Worker-local call log: `log.audit`, `log.tail`, `worker.log` | 0.4: `rook/worker/plugins/log.py`<br>0.5: `rook/core2/builtin/worker.yaml` | The worker's own record of what it ran, read from the worker rather than the hub journal. | 2 / 1 | Not covered |
| CAP-14 | Core worker caps: Worker description: `worker.description_get`, `worker.description_set` | 0.4: `rook/worker/core.py` | A short free-text role description for a worker ("the GPU box"), shown to agents; survives updates and moves. | 2 / 0 | Not covered |
| CAP-15 | Core worker caps: Custom shell caps: `customcap.*` | 0.4: `rook/worker/admin.py` | Define a named cap `cmd.<name>` from a shell template at runtime, without writing a plugin. | 3 / 0 | Not covered |
| CAP-16 | Core worker caps: Worker inbox: `msg.*` | 0.4: `rook/worker/plugins/msg.py` | Leave a text message in a worker's inbox, read it, clear it. | 3 / 0 | Not covered |
| CAP-17 | Plugins, config, updates: Plugin enable/disable/reload per worker and hub | 0.4: `rook/hub/node.py`, `rook/worker/admin.py` | Turn a plugin on or off on one worker or the hub, or reload it, without a restart. | 7 / 0 | Covered: PL3, P4 |
| CAP-18 | Plugins, config, updates: Plugin install and rollback on a worker: `core.plugin.install`, `core.plugin.rollback` | 0.5: `rook/core2/builtin/core.yaml` | Pull a hub-signed plugin bundle, install it, run its checks, roll back on failure; or go back a version. | 0 / 2 | Covered: PL3, PL4 |

### 3.2 MCP tools, 0.4

The 0.4 agent surface is up to 38 tools: 26 declared in `rook/band_mcp/server.py` and `claude_code.py`, and 12 handed over by hub plugins. Some rows below describe a behaviour of a tool, such as `rook_call` modes, rather than a separate tool.

| ID | What it is | Where | What it does | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MCP4-01 | rook_whoami | 0.4: rook/band_mcp/server.py:700 | Returns the identity (agent_id, key_id, kind, compound identity string) the MCP attributes the caller's calls to. | Not covered |
| MCP4-02 | rook_workers | 0.4: rook/band_mcp/server.py:713 + roster.py | Lists live workers, filtered by name / cap prefix / online, with a `fields` projection; adds hosting info from the `serves` hub plugin. | Covered: C2, B5 |
| MCP4-03 | rook_caps | 0.4: rook/band_mcp/server.py:734 + roster.py | Lists caps on the band and which workers hold them, by prefix / worker. | Covered: C2 |
| MCP4-04 | rook_call (core) | 0.4: rook/band_mcp/server.py:1016 | Calls a cap on a named worker (id or name, fuzzy), waits per the cap's declared timeout, journals it, returns compact `{ok,id,from,result}`. | Covered: C2, L1, W5, C3 |
| MCP4-05 | rook_call: required `why` | 0.4: server.py:1056 | Refuses a call without a one-line `why`; the why is journaled, shown on holds and written to the task whiteboard. | Covered: L1 |
| MCP4-06 | rook_call: refuses untargeted calls | 0.4: server.py:~1085 | A call with no worker is refused and the error lists the workers holding that cap ("first responder wins" never picks). | Not covered |
| MCP4-07 | rook_call: `{{secret:name}}` substitution | 0.4: server.py:~1108, vault.py | Substitutes vault values into args just before dispatch; the journal keeps the placeholder; unknown names fail. | Covered: X2 |
| MCP4-08 | rook_call: reply masking of vault values | 0.4: server.py:~1170, secret_mask.py | Every known vault value in the worker's reply is replaced with its `{{secret:name}}` stub before the agent sees it; `sensitive` hub caps (settings.fetch) skip masking and are not journaled. | Not covered |
| MCP4-09 | rook_call: `text=true` plain-text mode | 0.4: server.py:1046, envelope.call_text | Returns a successful result as plain text (shell.exec stdout/stderr/exit). | Not covered |
| MCP4-10 | rook_call: per-session cap tips / `hint` | 0.4: server.py:~1230, guidance.py | Attaches operator-editable `_tips` for the cap's prefix once per MCP session (again with hint=true). | Not covered |
| MCP4-11 | rook_call: unread-chat / task notices | 0.4: server.py:~1215, envelope.Notices | Piggybacks `_unread_chat`, `_task`, `_hygiene` notices on replies when new for the session. | Agents or Workbench |
| MCP4-12 | rook_call: console claim + commit/PR auto-link | 0.4: server.py:~1195-1210 | A terminal opened for a task is claimed for the caller and linked; commits/PRs seen in output are linked to tasks within the worker's band. | Agents or Workbench |
| MCP4-13 | Generated hub-cap tools (`tool=True`) | 0.4: rook/hub/mcp_tools.py register_cap_tools, called server.py:1954 | Mechanism: each hub cap declared `tool=True` gets an alias tool `rook_<cap>` calling rook_call on worker `rook`. In this tree no real cap sets tool=True (only the docstring example in rook/core/plugin.py:33), so it generates 0 tools. | Not covered (C2 says "Caps are discovered, not registered as tools") |
| MCP4-14 | Plugin-provided tools mechanism (`mcp_tools(invoke)`) | 0.4: rook/hub/mcp_tools.py register_plugin_tools, server.py:693 | Hub plugins hand over hand-shaped tools that call their caps in-process as the caller, with an approval-hold check on each inner cap. | Not covered (C2) |
| MCP4-15 | rook_secret list/set/delete | 0.4: server.py:1264, vault.py | Lists secret names+descriptions, stores/replaces one (and masks that value in existing journal rows), deletes one; each journaled as vault.*. | Covered: X1, X2, X3 |
| MCP4-16 | rook_secret get (raw value to agent) | 0.4: server.py:1288 | Returns the raw secret value to the agent; logged with identity and claimed task, journaled as vault.get. | Not covered (X3 says only users can view values) |
| MCP4-17 | rook_secret log (per-secret access log) | 0.4: server.py:1283, vault.access_log | Returns recent read/use/set/delete accesses for a secret. | Not covered |
| MCP4-18 | rook_journal | 0.4: server.py:1314, journal.py | Queries the call journal by call id (with full reply), worker, cap prefix, age, failures only. | Covered: C2, L2 |

### 3.3 MCP tools and doors, 0.5

The 0.5 MCP door serves 7 tools (`call`, `caps`, `workers`, `journal`, `holds`, `session`, `stream`). The HTTP door, OAuth front and web door sit beside it.

| ID | What it is | Where | What it does | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MCP5-01 | HTTP JSON door | 0.5: `rook/core2/hub/doors/http.py` (362 lines) | `POST /call`, `POST /session`, `POST /session/end`, `GET /workers`, `GET /caps`, `GET /journal`, `GET /health` (no token); bearer token; replies always `{ok}` | Not covered |
| MCP5-02 | HTTP holds routes | 0.5: `http.py` `GET /holds`, `POST /holds/<name>` | List holds and questions; approve/refuse or answer | Out by decision: "Approvals, security gates, elevation leases, change classes" |
| MCP5-03 | Plugin push route | 0.5: `POST /plugins/push {worker, plugin, why}` (owners) | Signs a library plugin for that worker and installs it | Covered: PL3, PL4 |
| MCP5-04 | Plugin HTTP routes | 0.5: `/p/<plugin><path>`; manifest `routes:` (`auth: user/hmac/token/none`); `HubPlugins.serve/route_allows` | Hub plugins add their own HTTP endpoints | Not covered |
| MCP5-05 | Desktop update feed | 0.5: `GET /updates/desktop.json` (no token); `updates.desktop_feed` | The desktop app's files from Rook's latest GitHub release, cached; serves the last good answer on failure | Covered: U4 |
| MCP5-06 | APK feed | 0.5: `rook/core2/hub/apkfeed.py`; `GET /apk.json[?channel=beta]`, `GET /apk` (no token) | Serves the phone app an owner put in `<data>/apk` (stable) or `<data>/apk-beta` after manifest, size and sha256 checks; beta only while newer than stable; 404 when empty | Covered: U4, A1 |
| MCP5-07 | Web door (site) | 0.5: `rook/core2/hub/doors/web/` | §1 above | Covered: P1 |
| MCP5-08 | MCP door | 0.5: `rook/core2/hub/doors/mcp.py` (356 lines) | Streamable HTTP (`POST /mcp` JSON-RPC; `GET` 405; stateless). Protocols 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18, 2025-03-26, 2024-11-05. Methods: `initialize`, `ping`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`. Bearer token; `origins` allowlist; `wait` (45 s); server INSTRUCTIONS text | Covered: C2 |
| MCP5-09 | MCP tool `call` | 0.5: `mcp.py TOOLS` | Runs one cap on one worker (or an appliance) with why, optional session and `elevate`; a held call answers `held` | Covered: C2 |
| MCP5-10 | MCP tool `caps` | 0.5: same | What workers serve, from manifests (offline included); `match` exact/prefix/glob; `full` | Covered: C2 |
| MCP5-11 | MCP tool `workers` | 0.5: same | The band's machines: name, id, plugins, description, problems | Covered: C2 |
| MCP5-12 | MCP tool `journal` | 0.5: same | Readable journal entries, newest first; filters incl. since/until/limit (≤500) | Covered: C2, L2 |
| MCP5-13 | MCP tool `holds` | 0.5: same | What waits on a person (held calls + `approval.ask` questions); yours, or all for an owner | Out by decision: "Approvals, security gates, elevation leases, change classes" |
| MCP5-14 | MCP tool `session` | 0.5: same | Start a session narrowed to cap patterns, tied to a project/task; or end one | Not covered |
| MCP5-15 | MCP tool `stream` | 0.5: same | Read a stream from a cursor (long poll `wait`) or write input to it | Covered: C2 |
| MCP5-16 | MCP stdio shim | 0.5: `rook/core2/hub/doors/mcp_stdio.py` | One JSON-RPC line on stdin → POST to the MCP door; token from `ROOK_TOKEN` or `--token-file`; requests run side by side | Not covered |
| MCP5-17 | mDNS hub discovery | 0.5: `rook/core2/enroll/mdns.py`; hub.yaml `enroll: {discover: true, url}` | DNS-SD `_rook-hub._tcp` responder (PTR/SRV/TXT `url=`, `hub=`); device-side `discover()` | Not covered |

The 29 dashboard views map to these rows: Feed `PAGE4-018`, Overview `-020`, Start page `-021`, Work `-028`, Huddles `-030`, Sessions `-031`, Knowledge `-035`, Jobs `-037` (tabs `-036` to `-043`), Memory `-044`, Home agent `-045`, Roles `-046`, Model providers `-047`, Voice `-048`, Agent instructions `-049`, Trust map `-050`, Approvals `-051`, Decisions `-052`, People `-053`, Account & access `-055` (to `-061`), API tokens `-062`, Secrets `-065`, Bands `-066`, Settings `-073`, Workers `-084`, Install a worker `-110`, Plugins `-113`, Updates `-116`, Claude Code `-125` and Help `-126`. The pages served outside the dashboard are `PAGE4-127` to `PAGE4-137`. Every other row is a feature inside one of these pages.

| ID | What it is | Where | What it does | Status |

Each of the 29 views is an ES module, loaded the first time its `#view` is opened. | Covered: P1 (differs: vanilla JS modules, not Vue 3) |

| HUB4-001 | Per-call attribution | 0.4: rook/band_mcp/attribution.py, server.py:286 | Every tool call is attributed from the bearer token; invalid/revoked token denies the call; if the checker itself crashes the call proceeds "unverified" with an alert row. | Covered: L1, T4 (fail-open: see differs) |

### 3.6 Worker core and the `rook` CLI, 0.4

| ID | What it is | Where | What it does | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WRK4-001 | Worker boot / CLI entry | 0.4: rook/worker/cli.py (`rook-worker`, `rook worker`, `python -m rook.worker`) | Parses flags/env, resolves hub+PSK from enrollment, pushed config or flags, builds transport and Worker, runs forever | Covered: W1, W2 |
| WRK4-002 | Worker flags: --hub/--psk/--ws/--ws-url/--bind-port/--keepalive/--name/--enable | 0.4: rook/worker/cli.py | Manual band join with a shared band PSK, transport choice (UDP or WS via hub /band bridge), plugin allow-list, name | Covered: R1 (differs: PSK, not keypair) |
| WRK4-003 | `--selftest` / `--version` | 0.4: rook/worker/cli.py | Load all plugins offline and print version + cap count; used as the pre-swap smoke test | Covered: U2, U3 |
| WRK4-004 | `--install-cli` / desktop launcher | 0.4: rook/worker/desktop.py, cli.py | Installs a `rook` launcher from the bundle and adds it to PATH in .profile/.bashrc/.zshrc (marker block); `--cli` opens the bundled terminal dashboard | Not covered |
| WRK4-005 | State dir + ROOK_WORKER_HOME migration | 0.4: rook/core/paths.py, cli.py | State (identity, enrollment, config, audit, plugin data, ban flag) in ~/.rook-band-worker or ROOK_WORKER_HOME; copies old state once; ROOK_WORKER_HOME_FRESH=1 for a separate worker | Covered: W7 (state survives reinstall) |
| WRK4-006 | Saved-state-beats-flags warning, auto_start | 0.4: rook/worker/cli.py | Enrollment/pushed config override --hub/--psk and log that they did; enrollment `auto_start` implies `--enrolled` | Not covered |
| WRK4-007 | Self-minted stable worker_id | 0.4: rook/worker/core.py `stable_worker_id` | Worker generates its own uuid4 hex and persists it so restarts keep one identity and bans stick | Covered: W7 (differs: worker mints, plan says hub mints GUID+moniker) |
| WRK4-008 | Announce loop (heartbeat) | 0.4: rook/worker/core.py `announce`, `_announce_loop` | Sends `kind:announce` every 30 s (±20% jitter) and on every (re)connect: worker_id, name, description, caps, caps_hash, features, plugins, version, build, app_release, facts, tiers, authz readiness, plugin hb, device ids | Covered: H1, H2 (differs) |
| WRK4-009 | Hash-only announces | 0.4: rook/worker/core.py, wire/envelope.py (ROOK_ANNOUNCE_CAPS_HASH) | Opt-in: omit caps when unchanged, full caps every 10th announce, on change, after reconnect | Not covered (H1 says every heartbeat carries full state) |
| WRK4-010 | Announce interval set locally | 0.4: rook/worker/cli.py `--announce-interval`, wconfig `announce_interval` | Interval comes from the worker flag or a pushed config override, not from a hub reply on connect | Covered: H2 (differs) |
| WRK4-011 | Per-plugin heartbeat blocks (`hb`) | 0.4: rook/core/host.py `heartbeats()`; plugins battery, adb, webos, devkit, homeassistant, proc, terminals, sessions, agenthost, browser, selfupdate | Each plugin may add a small live-status dict to the announce (battery %, TV on/app, live terminals, harnesses, update state) | Covered: H1, H4 (differs: no fixed CPU/RAM/disk block) |
| WRK4-012 | Facts detection + placement | 0.4: rook/core/facts.py `detect_facts`, `local_facts`, `evaluate_placement`; NodeFacts | Detects OS, arch, GPU, machine id, embedded, app=android; plugins are placed by these facts (hub-only via signed is_hub grant) | Covered: H1, PL3 |
| WRK4-013 | Machine id fact | 0.4: rook/core/facts.py | Reports /etc/machine-id (or equivalent) so the hub can tell which workers share its host | Not covered |
| WRK4-014 | Cap dispatch | 0.4: rook/worker/core.py `_on_message` | Parses requests, ignores calls for other targets, answers unknown-cap only when targeted, rejects non-object args, maps CapTimeout/TypeError/CapError to plain error replies | Covered: W2, W5, C3 |
| WRK4-015 | Default cap timeout 300 s | 0.4: rook/core/registry.py, plugin.py `default_cap_timeout`, ROOK_CAP_TIMEOUT | Abandons any cap call after 300 s unless the cap opts out or takes a timeout arg | Out by decision: Build rules, "No gates, limits, expiries, approvals or review processes until core is stable. Hardening is a later pass." |
| WRK4-016 | Cap output contract (limit/fields) | 0.4: rook/core/registry.py | Registry enforces a cap's declared default page size and field list at dispatch | Not covered |
| WRK4-017 | Pushed secret resolution | 0.4: rook/worker/core.py `resolve_secret_refs`, wconfig `unresolved` | After connect, worker asks hub cap `settings.worker_secret` for `{{secret:name}}` values in pushed env; retries at 2/10/30/60 s; values kept in memory only | Covered: CF3, X2 (differs) |
